## M9 scalable economy idempotency correction

The earlier v0.9.0 candidate's 2,048-entry `RecentEventIds` hard stop is superseded and is not an accepted delivery contract. That build stopped every otherwise-supported economy aggregate after its identity list filled, disabled Money, Cash, and route economy capabilities, and could leave lifetime, active-run, segment, starting-map, and route-map totals permanently incomplete at normal transaction volume. Its implementation commit, package identity, deployment proof, cold-launch proof, 589-test count, and draft-delivery claim are retained only as superseded evidence. The separate M8 limit of 2,048 `SegmentEventAssociation` rows per run remains out of scope; it may disable general event-association evidence, but it must not stop economy reduction or otherwise-supported economy map totals.

The production lifecycle trace establishes the following ordering and replay boundaries. `NativeEconomyAdapter` creates each normalized flow synchronously under one random activation ID and a strictly increasing positive sequence. It then attempts the profile and active-run destinations independently, in that order, before another adapter event ID is created. `NativeProfileCoordinator` mutates the in-memory lifetime profile and marks its single-flight profile writer dirty. Active-raid flows independently mutate the active run and event-time segment, dirtying the coalesced checkpoint. An ordinary profile write is allowed only after the current active-run checkpoint revision is durable; a failed checkpoint blocks the profile write, and failed profile writes retain their dirty revision for retry. The deferred profile state stores the exact active-run economy already applied to lifetime. After abrupt termination, recovery selects the first structurally and semantically valid active-checkpoint primary, backup, or temporary candidate, subtracts that durable lifetime watermark from checkpoint totals, merges only the non-negative exact difference, finalizes the interrupted run once, durably saves the profile, and then deletes all three checkpoint candidates. If the run ID already exists, the stale finalized checkpoint is cleared without applying either lifetime or completed-run totals again. Completed-run, starting-map, and route-map fan-out is guarded by the persisted run ID before any aggregate merge. Save-generation validation prevents an archived or rotated generation's flow or checkpoint from entering the new generation.

The corrected design uses an activation-scoped monotonic replay cursor on each directly recording economy aggregate. The cursor stores only the current producer activation ID and the greatest sequence whose delivery window is closed for that aggregate. Registration persists the new activation with closed-through sequence zero before its first positive-sequence event; that state is valid and must survive cloning, persistence, and restart without being labelled repaired. Accepting sequence `N` closes every lower sequence for that aggregate: because ID creation and destination publication are synchronous and ordered, a lower sequence not previously delivered to that aggregate cannot become a valid first delivery later. It is therefore either a duplicate/stale replay or violates the verified adapter ordering contract and is rejected. Sequence gaps are supported without storing them; base-only events, other runs, and earlier segments can legitimately consume those numbers before a particular run or segment first observes the activation. A different activation cannot enter a non-empty active aggregate. At profile open, interrupted recovery runs before a newly created adapter activation is registered. Registration preserves the cursor when the same activation reopens a slot in-process and replaces a dead activation only after its surviving checkpoint primary, backup, and temporary artifacts have been consumed, deleted, or archived. This provides exact replay rejection with constant metadata per aggregate rather than one persisted identity per transaction.

Current schema-9 candidate compatibility remains in schema 9. Unsaturated legacy `RecentEventIds` are validated and retained through backup/checkpoint candidate selection, then removed only at the post-recovery safe boundary above. A legacy `DeduplicationSaturated` marker preserves all recorded exact totals, becomes an explicit legacy-incomplete-history marker, and no longer disables future capture after the dead activation's recovery artifacts are gone. Valid old candidates may omit the new cursor. New candidates with a present but malformed activation/sequence cursor are rejected before normalization so a valid backup or temporary candidate can win. Normalization is idempotent. Replay cursors do not participate in completed-run aggregate merging or checkpoint-minus-profile arithmetic: those boundaries use run identity and exact aggregate composition, respectively. Money and Cash arithmetic overflow remains independent and continues to retain prior exact values while disabling only the unrepresentable currency.

Required corrective validation includes more than 2,048 Money events, more than 2,048 Cash events, a mixed stream, a 100,000-event lifetime stress, fixed-size replay metadata, stable serialized profile size apart from counter/revision digits and legitimate breakdowns, duplicate delivery on both sides of checkpoint/profile durability, write failure/retry, newer-checkpoint and newer-profile recovery, repeated recovery, finalized and interrupted checkpoint windows, multiple activations, save rotation, sequence gaps and stale out-of-order rejection, old unsaturated and saturated schema-9 candidates, malformed primary/backup/temporary selection, exact run/segment/starting-map/route-map composition independent of the remaining route-association ceiling, projection agreement, independent arithmetic overflow, no per-event durable profile write, O(1) clean economy tick, and no M1-M8.1 regression.

The correction passes 606/606 tests in both development and complete Release runs. `OneHundredThousandLifetimeEventsKeepConstantReplayMetadata` and `SerializedProfileSizeDoesNotGrowWithHistoricalEconomyIdentities` prove exact 50,000/50,000 Money/Cash composition, an empty historical ID list, one activation/sequence cursor, and no serialized growth proportional to transaction count. Separate 4,096-event Money and Cash regressions retain supported capture. A 5,000-event two-segment run continues exact run, segment, and starting-map totals after the independent route-association ceiling disables route-map evidence for that run; 2,500 one-event runs separately prove starting-map and route-map economy aggregates beyond the legacy identity limit without approaching the per-run association bound. Deferred-profile/checkpoint tests reject duplicates on both sides of durability, recover only checkpoint-minus-watermark totals, repeat recovery without reapplication, retry a failed bounded profile snapshot, and prove legacy evidence is not compacted until the checkpoint has been recovered and its primary/backup/temporary artifacts are deleted. Schema-9 tests preserve unsaturated and old-saturated totals, mark the latter explicitly incomplete, resume under a new activation, and choose valid backup/temporary candidates over malformed replay cursors in both profile and active-run stores. The installed Duckov contract probe, frame-time analyzer, warning-free Core/native Release builds, formatter, source-safety audit, and exact five-file package-directory validation pass.

### M9 post-sequence-zero review corrections

The sequence-zero package and minimal cold persistence check above remain valid historical evidence for that boundary, but later review supersedes ZIP SHA-256 `d2fa07eb39c2edb46bf0c42a2bf763798f2913751b75bb9742446ab4cff5b217` as the final M9 candidate. Three defects were confirmed. First, production `Inventory.AddAndMerge` can consume a dropped Cash item into a different compatible stack before `OnPickupSuccess`; the old reconciliation then found no picked runtime ID in the owned snapshot and fabricated the full increase as a new external acquisition. Second, `OnLevelBeginInitializing` reset a dirty Cash baseline without reconciling it, so a corpse/container transfer immediately before transition could be lost. Third, checkpoint-minus-watermark recovery treated a saturation-only difference as empty, so a crash after durable checkpoint saturation but before profile durability could reopen lifetime Money as supported.

Implementation commit `20fa8acdc873e331515d9155ed838b96cb820b7a` retains each removed player-originated Cash identity with its last exact owned amount; merged re-pickups therefore remain exact `UnknownAdjustment` inflows with `ProvenExternalRaidAcquisition=false` even when the picked item is absent and has stack count zero after the production merge. Level initialization now flushes dirty Cash under the captured pre-transition run/map/segment context before baseline suspension and hydration. Deferred economy recovery treats a newly saturated Money or Cash flag as a recoverable checkpoint delta even when all counters equal the persisted watermark, merges the narrow capability degradation once, retains the prior exact `Int64` maximum, and remains idempotent after clean close.

`DropRepickupConsumedByAddAndMergeIsNotAcquiredAgain` models the production merge order with a different surviving stack and a consumed picked item. `SceneInitializationFlushesPendingCashBeforeSuspendingTheBaseline` enters initialization before the next coalesced tick and proves the exact old-context delta is emitted once. `DeferredArithmeticSaturationRecoversFromCheckpointWhenProfileStillHasTheExactMaximum` persists an unsaturated `Int64.MaxValue` profile/watermark, durably writes a newer saturated checkpoint, reopens without a profile save, and proves lifetime plus completed-run degradation and repeated-reopen idempotency. The focused 20-test gate and complete Debug/Release suites pass; the new total is 609/609 in each configuration.
